Formal proof in comments:
For any valid nonnegative array a length n with S=sum a, B=max b. Zero-lag term b[n-1] = sum_i a_i^2 (in np.convolve full, index n-1). By Cauchy S^2 <= n sum squares = n b0. Score = 2 n B / S^2 >= 2 n b0 / S^2 >=2. Equality iff all a_i equal (and B=b0). Thus [1.0]*n optimal. Use n = parent length if available else 1.

```
import time
import numpy as np

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    # The objective is scale invariant. For any admissible clipped sequence a of
    # length n, the zero-lag convolution coefficient is sum(a*a). By Cauchy,
    # 2n max(b)/sum(a)^2 >= 2n sum(a*a)/sum(a)^2 >= 2. Equality is attained by
    # any constant nonzero sequence. We therefore return a constant candidate
    # (matching the parent length when available) and verify it with evaluator.
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    n = 6121
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            arr = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            if arr.size > 0 and np.isfinite(arr).all():
                # Use parent's length if it is reasonable; otherwise keep 6121.
                if 1 <= arr.size <= 200000: # or cap 6121?
                    n = int(arr.size)
        except Exception:
            pass
    # But if parent huge, n huge would be bad. Better cap to min(parent, 6121?) Wait if parent size 6121, cap 6121. If parent size smaller? use smaller? Could use arr.size if >0. But if parent size 1e6, cap. Set n = min(arr.size, 6121) maybe; if no parent, 6121. If parent size 10, return length 10? That's okay. But if parent size 1e6, return 6121. Good.
```

Implementation:
```
def _eval_candidate(c):
   try:
      s = float(evaluate_sequence(c))
      return s if np.isfinite(s) else None
   except Exception:
      return None
```

Let's formulate final code:
```python
import time
import numpy as np

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    # For any nonnegative sequence a of length n, the zero lag of a*a is
    # sum(a_i^2), hence by Cauchy-Schwarz
    # 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum a)^2 >= 2.
    # A constant sequence attains 2. Return such a candidate.
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1300.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget < 0:
        budget = 1300.0

    # Choose a safe length: prefer the length of the supplied construction,
    # but never above 6121 to keep the exact evaluator's convolution cheap.
    n = 6121
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            if p.size > 0 and np.isfinite(p).all():
                n = max(1, min(int(p.size), 6121))
        except Exception:
            n = 6121

    # If the remaining time is suspiciously small, use the smallest valid length.
    now = time.monotonic()
    if budget < 2.0 and n > 1:
        n = 1

    best = [1.0] * n
    try:
        score = float(evaluate_sequence(best))
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            best = [1.0]
            score = float(evaluate_sequence(best))
    except Exception:
        best = [1.0]

    return [float(x) for x in best]
```

Final code:
```python
import time
import numpy as np

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    # ... comments proof
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1300.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget < 0.0:
        budget = 1300.0

    # If there is essentially no time, use the minimal valid length.
    n = 1
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            if p.size > 0:
                n = max(1, min(int(p.size), 6121))
        except Exception:
            n = 1
    if parent is None:
        # Use the announced construction length for a nontrivial but still cheap candidate.
        n = 6121
    if budget < 1.0 and n > 1:
        n = 1

    best = [1.0] * n
    # Verify with the authoritative evaluator; if that is somehow impossible,
    # the one-element constant remains valid by inspection.
    try:
        score = float(evaluate_sequence(best))
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            best = [1.0]
    except Exception:
        best = [1.0]
    return [float(x) for x in best]
```

Algorithm A (smooth multiplicative gradient):
- Normalize p to sum1 (or sqrt(2n)). Start with parent or random.
- Compute c=p*p via FFT; M=max c. If score < best update.
- Choose temperature τ. Weights w_k = exp((c_k - M)/τ). Normalize sum1.
- Compute g_i = ∑_j p_j w_{i+j} via convolution with reversed w.
- Update p_i ← p_i * exp(-η g_i), then normalize. Need choose η such that objective decreases. Since gradient of logsumexp Fτ = logsumexp(c/τ)? Let's set w=exp((c-M)/τ) (normalized). Our g_i = (H_w p)_i? ∑_k w_k p_{k-i}? equals ∑_j p_j w_{i+j}. Good. So gradient = 2g (times 1? times 1/τ if w normalized? Wait w normalized from exp(c/τ), derivative of logsumexp c/τ = (1/τ)∑ w_k * 2H_k p. So factor 1/τ. In code, can absorb into η.
- Multiplicative gradient on simplex: p ← p * exp(-η * 2g/τ) / sum. For linear objective on positive simplex, multiplicative update converges. For nonconvex, line search.

We can approximate g by solving a maximum-density problem: We want nonnegative g maximizing sum subject f*g_k≤M. This is equivalent to finding a "longest path"? - Variables j (n) with potential u_j = ∑_k y_k A_{k,j}; constraints k with v_k = ∑_j x_j A_{k,j}.

Let's design a robust first-order solver for T(f):
- Variables x in simplex. Use multiplicative or projected gradient.
- Objective L(x)=max_k ℓ_k, ℓ=f*x.
- Subgradient for active set (weights w) is d_j = ∑_k w_k f_{k-j}. The SSE same.
- Use a smoothing/logsumexp to get continuous gradient: Fτ(x)=logsumexp(ℓ/τ). Gradient as above. We can do L-BFGS? Multiplicative focuses.
- Since L convex, we can use a subgradient method with decreasing steps and Harada? Multiplicative often good for simplex.
- We can also use "best response" oracle: Given x, find active constraint with max load; update x toward variable j that minimizes A^T y? In saddle point game, variables x and dual y. This can be very efficient:
For min_x max_k ℓ_k, define dual y over k maximizing min_j? The saddle game: primal x lower? Actually min_{x∈Δ} max_{y∈Δ} y^T A x. This is a zero-sum matrix game. We can use multiplicative weights for games:
Initialize x,y.
- Primal best response to y: j minimizing ∑_k y_k A_{k,j}. Increase x on that j.
- Dual best response to x: k maximizing ∑_j x_j A_{k,j}. Increase y on that k.
- Update x_j ∝ x_j exp(-η ∑ y A_{k,j}), y_k ∝ y_k exp(η ∑ x A_{k,j}). This is Hellman's algorithm. Converges in O(log n / ε^2). We can compute the cost vectors via FFT each step. This may solve T(f) much faster than gradient, with global convergence (approx) because convex-concave. It also naturally maintains distributions. Great!

Algorithm for T(f):
Given nonnegative f. We need find x (and y) solving min_x max_k (f*x)_k. This is a matrix game payoff A_{k,j}=f_{k-j} if valid else0. Both strategies nonnegative. We can run multiplicative weights:
- x over n variables, y over K=2n-1 constraints, start x=f normalized, y maybe uniform or based on f*f.
- For iteration:
   * Compute load_from_x = A x (K) = f*x via FFT. (For y best response and dual gradient)
   * Compute cost_to_x = A^T y (n) = ∑ y_k f_{k-j} via FFT (same as correlation f,y).
   * Update x_j *= exp(-η_x * cost_to_x_j), normalize. (Primal minimizes expected payoff)
   * Update y_k *= exp(η_y * load_from_x_k), normalize. (Dual maximizes)
This is a standard mirror descent in game. The primal objective value for x will decrease. We can stop when max load and min_j cost? In game, value v≈ average payoff. For T(f), we care about x. We can run e.g., 100-500 iterations per outer; each iteration two FFTs (or three: f*x and f^T*y; F_f cached, F_x and F_y? load = f*x needs F_f*F_x; cost = f^T*y needs F_f*F_y_rev? Same as q with y). We can cache F_f. Each iteration: F_x rfft, F_y rfft; two iffts/correlations. Actually load f*x: irfft(F_f*F_x). cost f^T y: r=y reversed; irfft(F_f*F_r). Update x,y. So 2 forward +2 inverse per iter plus maybe. L=32768, 500 iter ~2000 FFT, okay.
- After inner iterations, x approximate T(f). The value V=max load_from_x or payoff; also min cost? At equilibrium, max load ≈ min cost ≈ value. We can use x.

This game algorithm is elegant and likely robust. It solves convex T(f) (in x) and yields x/y. We can use it as inner solver. Then outer update f with x.

If x optimal normalized sum1, the max sum S_g = M/v. Then rescale g to normalization S_f: g' = S_f/S_g * g = (S_f v/M) * x. If S_f=S_f, direction g' has sum S_f. Linear update f'=(1-t)f + t g'. Since f and g' same sum, scale invariant. The direction coefficient relative to x is t*(S_f v/M); but since f update scale invariant, multiplying g' by a constant less than1 just reduces step size t. So using x directly with a chosen t is equivalent with adjusted t. Good.

   # For x update, need cost = A^T y. F_y_rev = rfft(y_rev,L); cost_full=irfft(Ff*F_y_rev,L); cost=cost_full[idx]

   Update y = y * exp(eta_y * (load - load.mean()) / load_range); normalize.

Detailed code structure:
```
import time, math, numpy as np

def _next_pow2(m):
    L=1
    while L<m: L<<=1
    return L

class Optimizer:
    def __init__(self,n,budget,deadline):
       self.n=n; self.K=2*n-1; L=_next_pow2(3*n-2); self.L=L
       self.idx = self.K-1 - np.arange(n)
    def load(self,Fa, B):
       return np.fft.irfft(Fa * np.fft.rfft(B, self.L), self.L)[:self.K]
    def opp(self,Ff, y):
       r = y[::-1]
       return np.fft.irfft(Ff * np.fft.rfft(r, self.L), self.L)[self.idx]
    def score(self,p):
       Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,self.L); c=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp,self.L)[:self.K]; return 2.0*self.n*c.max(), c
    def game_step(self,Ff, x, y, eta=0.5):
       Fx=np.fft.rfft(x,self.L); load=np.fft.irfft(Fx*Ff,self.L)[:self.K]
       # update y on max load
       yl = load - load.mean()
       rng = np.max(np.abs(yl))
       if rng > 1e-15:
           y = y * np.exp(eta*yl/rng); y/=y.sum()
       # cost for x
       cost = self.opp(Ff, y)
       xl = cost - cost.mean()
       rn = np.max(np.abs(xl))
       if rn > 1e-15:
           x = x * np.exp(-eta*xl/rn); x/=x.sum()
       return x,y,load
```
But in game, x update should use cost before y update or after? Standard: update both with current opponent; order: compute load and cost, update x then y. We can update x first (min) then y (max) using old x. But then load used for y is old. Fine. Or y first. Let's do:
- compute load from x, cost from y.
- update x using cost (eta_x)
- update y using load (eta_y)
This uses old x/y consistently.
Need maybe use different eta: xeta=0.5, yet=0.5.

Pseudo for `solve_game`:
```
def solve_game(Ff, f, x, y, max_iters, eta, deadline):
    # x,y normalized
    best_primal = np.inf
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       Fx = rfft(x,L)
       load = irfft(Fx*Ff,L)[:K]
       # cost uses y
       Fr_y = rfft(y[::-1],L)
       cost = irfft(Fr_y*Ff,L)[idx]
       # update x
       xl = cost - cost.mean(); rng = max(abs(xl))
       if rng>0:
          x = x * exp(-eta*xl/rng); x/=x.sum()
       # update y
       yl = load - load.mean(); rngl=max(abs(yl))
       if rngl>0:
          y = y * exp(eta*yl/rngl); y/=y.sum()
       # optional: track max_load
    return x,y
```
Order: If update x before y using old y, and y using old x, consistent. Good.

But the primal objective after x update is not load from new x, but next iteration. We can track `load_max` for new x if needed.

```
           p=q; current_score=qscore; current_c=qc; accepted=True; break
   if not accepted:
      # maybe use x if it improves
      xscore,xc = score_with_c(x_state)
      if xscore < current_score - 1e-12:
          p=x_state; current_score=xscore; current_c=xc; accepted=True
   if not accepted:
      # accept a tiny move toward x if not too bad?

      q=...; qscore,qc=score_with_c(q)
      if qscore <= current_score * 1.01 + 1e-12:

      else:

          current_score,current_c=score_with_c(p)
   if current_score < best_score: update best add
   # maybe if best_score < 1.502 and time small? continue

```

```
def clean_positive(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = a.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return np.ones(n)/n
    a = a / s
    # add small floor to avoid zeros? For final zeros okay; for optimization floor 1e-14
    a[a < 1e-15] = 1e-15
    a /= a.sum()
    return a
```

Helper class:
```
class ConvSolver:
   def __init__(self,n):
      self.n=n; self.K=2*n-1; self.L=1; m=3*n-2; while L<m: L*=2
      self.idx = self.K-1 - np.arange(n)
   def conv(self, Ff, x):
      return np.fft.irfft(Ff*np.fft.rfft(x,self.L), self.L)[:self.K]
   def cost(self, Ff, y):
      r = y[::-1]
      return np.fft.irfft(Ff*np.fft.rfft(r,self.L), self.L)[self.idx]
   def square(self,p):
      Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,self.L)
      c=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp,self.L)[:self.K]
      c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
      return 2.0*self.n*c.max(), c, Fp
```

```
def smooth_random(n,rng,scale=0.7,n_freq=10):
   i=np.arange(n)
   s=np.ones(n)
   for m in range(1,n_freq+1):
```

```
    def game_solve(self, Ff, x, y, max_iters, eta=0.5, gap_tol=1e-7, deadline=None):
        x=x.copy(); y=y.copy()
        gap=np.inf
        rng = np.random.rand? no
        for it in range(max_iters):
            if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() > deadline: break
            Fx = np.fft.rfft(x,self.L)
            load = np.fft.irfft(Fx*Ff,self.L)[:self.K]
            load = np.clip(load,0,None)
            Fr = np.fft.rfft(y[::-1],self.L)
            cost = np.fft.irfft(Fr*Ff,self.L)[self.idx]
            xl = cost - cost.mean()
            rngx = np.max(np.abs(xl))
            if rngx > 1e-18:
                x = x * np.exp(-eta * xl / rngx)
                x /= x.sum()
            yl = load - load.mean()
            rngy = np.max(np.abs(yl))
            if rngy > 1e-18:
                y = y * np.exp(eta * yl / rngy)
                y /= y.sum()
            if (it & 31) == 31:
                # recompute gap? using old values is okay but after updates stale; recompute cheap? We can use current load/cost old.
                gap = load.max() - cost.min()
                if gap <= gap_tol:
                    break
        return x,y,gap
```

Now, code for `optimize_start`:
```
    def optimize_start(self,p0,deadline,pool):
        p=self.clean(p0)
        score,c,Ff=self.score_p(p)
        best_score=score; best_p=p.copy()
        pool.add(p,score)
        current_score=score; current_c=c; current_Ff=Ff
        x_state=p.copy()
        stagn=0
        while time.monotonic() < deadline:
            if not np.isfinite(current_score): break
            y=self.init_y(current_c, current_score)
            # inner iters: if current_score near target, maybe 120, else 80
            inner = 100
            x_state,y,gap,v = self.game_solve(current_Ff, x_state, y, inner, 0.55, deadline)
            M = current_score / (2.0*self.n)
            scale = v/M if M>0 else 1.0
            scale = float(np.clip(scale,0.05,1.0))
            ts = [0.05, 0.15*scale, 0.35*scale, 0.7*scale, 1.0]
            best_cand=None
            for t in ts:
                if not (0.0 < t <=1.0): continue
                q = self.clean((1.0-t)*p + t*x_state)
                qs,qc,qF = self.score_p(q)
                if best_cand is None or qs < best_cand[0]: best_cand=(qs,q,qc,qF,t)
            if best_cand is not None:
                qs,q,qc,qF,t = best_cand
                if qs < current_score - 1e-12:
                   p=q; current_score=qs; current_c=qc; current_Ff=qF; stagn=0
                   if qs<best_score: best_score=qs; best_p=p.copy(); pool.add(p,best_score)
                elif qs <= current_score + 1e-2*current_score + 1e-12:
                   # allow slight worsening to escape
                   p=q; current_score=qs; current_c=qc; current_Ff=qF; stagn+=1
                   if qs<best_score: update
                else:
                   stagn += 2
                   if stagn % 8 == 0:
                      noise = np.exp(self.rng.normal(0.0,0.05+self.n, size=n)?); p=clean(p*noise); score...
           if stagn>12:
              # stronger restart from best_p
              p=clean(np.exp(self.rng.normal(0.0,0.15,size=n))*best_p)
              current_score,current_c,current_Ff=score_p(p); x_state=p.copy(); stagn=0
        return best_p,best_score
```

If x=p, qs=current <= current+1%, accepted with stagnation++ every loop, causing infinite small no-op. Need avoid accepting a candidate that does not move p.
```python
import time
import math
import numpy as np


def _next_pow2_ge(m):
    L = 1
    while L < m:
        L <<= 1
    return L


def _finite_list(a):
    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if a.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(a).all():
            return None
        return a
    except Exception:
        return None


class _Pool:
    def __init__(self, max_items=15):
        self.max_items = int(max_items)
        self.items = []

    def add(self, p, score):
        try:
            score = float(score)
        except Exception:
            return
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            return
        p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if p.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(p).all():
            return
        self.items.append((score, p.copy()))
        if len(self.items) > self.max_items:
            self.items.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
            self.items = self.items[: self.max_items]

    def top(self, k):
        arr = sorted(self.items, key=lambda z: z[0])
        return arr[: max(0, int(k))]


class _ConvOptimizer:
    def __init__(self, n, seed=42):
        self.n = int(n)
        self.K = 2 * self.n - 1
        self.L = _next_pow2_ge(3 * self.n - 2)
        self.idx = self.K - 1 - np.arange(self.n, dtype=np.int64)
        self.rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    def clean(self, a):
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if a.size != self.n:
            if a.size > self.n:
                a = a[: self.n]
            else:
                fill = float(a.mean()) if a.size else 1.0
                if not np.isfinite(fill):
                    fill = 1.0 / self.n
                a = np.concatenate([a, np.full(self.n - a.size, fill, dtype=np.float64)])
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            a = np.ones(self.n, dtype=np.float64) / self.n
            return a
        a = a / s
        a[a < 1e-15] = 1e-15
        a = a / a.sum()
        if not np.isfinite(a).all():
            a = np.ones(self.n, dtype=np.float64) / self.n
        return a

    def score_p(self, p):
        p = self.clean(p)
        Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, self.L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(Fp * Fp, self.L)[: self.K]
        c = np.maximum(np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
        return float(2.0 * self.n * float(c.max())), c, Fp

    def init_y(self, c, score):
        c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if c.size != self.K:
            return np.ones(self.K, dtype=np.float64) / self.K
        m = float(score) / (2.0 * self.n) if score > 0 else float(c.max())
        m = max(m, 1e-300)
        tau = max(0.05 * m, 1e-300)
        u = (c - m) / tau
        u -= float(u.max())
        y = np.exp(np.clip(u, -50.0, 50.0))
        s = float(y.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            y = np.ones(self.K, dtype=np.float64) / self.K
        else:
            y = y / s
        return y

    def game_solve(self, Ff, x, y, max_iters, eta, deadline):
        n, K, L, idx = self.n, self.K, self.L, self.idx
        x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        x = self.clean(x)
        y = np.clip(y, 0.0, None)
        ys = float(y.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(ys) or ys <= 0.0:
            y = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64) / K
        else:
            y = y / ys

        gap = math.inf
        v = None
        for it in range(int(max_iters)):
            if time.monotonic() > float(deadline):
                break

            Fx = np.fft.rfft(x, L)
            load = np.fft.irfft(Fx * Ff, L)[:K]
            load = np.maximum(load, 0.0)

            Fr_y = np.fft.rfft(y[::-1], L)
            cost = np.fft.irfft(Fr_y * Ff, L)[idx]
            cost = np.maximum(cost, 0.0)

            xl = cost - float(cost.mean())
            rx = float(np.max(np.abs(xl)))
            if rx > 1e-18:
                x = x * np.exp(np.clip(-eta * xl / rx, -8.0, 8.0))
                xs = float(x.sum())
                if np.isfinite(xs) and xs > 0.0:
                    x = x / xs
                else:
                    x = self.clean(x)

            yl = load - float(load.mean())
            ry = float(np.max(np.abs(yl)))
            if ry > 1e-18:
                y = y * np.exp(np.clip(eta * yl / ry, -8.0, 8.0))
                ys = float(y.sum())
                if np.isfinite(ys) and ys > 0.0:
                    y = y / ys
                else:
                    y = self.init_y(load, 2.0 * n * float(load.max()))

            if (it & 31) == 31:
                gap = float(load.max() - cost.min())
                gap_tol = max(1e-9, 1e-4 * float(load.max()))
                if gap <= gap_tol:
                    break

        if v is None:
            Fx = np.fft.rfft(x, self.L)
            load = np.fft.irfft(Fx * Ff, self.L)[:K]
            load = np.maximum(load, 0.0)
            v = float(load.max())
        return x, y, gap, v

    def optimize_start(self, p0, deadline, pool):
        p = self.clean(p0)
        score, c, Ff = self.score_p(p)
        best_score, best_p = score, p.copy()
        pool.add(p, best_score)

        current_score, current_c, current_Ff = score, c, Ff
        x_state = p.copy()
        stagnation = 0

        while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
            if not np.isfinite(current_score):
                break

            y = self.init_y(current_c, current_score)
            inner_iters = 120 if current_score > 1.7 else 180
            x_state, y, gap, v = self.game_solve(
                current_Ff, x_state, y, inner_iters, 0.55, deadline
            )

            M = current_score / (2.0 * self.n)
            if M > 0.0 and np.isfinite(v):
                scale = float(np.clip(v / M, 0.05, 1.0))
            else:
                scale = 1.0

            try_t = [0.05, 0.15 * scale, 0.35 * scale, 0.7 * scale, 1.0]
            best_cand = None
            for t in try_t:
                if not (0.0 < t <= 1.0):
                    continue
                q = self.clean((1.0 - t) * p + t * x_state)
                qs, qc, qF = self.score_p(q)
                if best_cand is None or qs < best_cand[0]:
                    best_cand = (qs, q, qc, qF, t)

            improved = False
            if best_cand is not None:
                qs, q, qc, qF, t = best_cand
                moved = bool(np.mean(np.abs(q - p)) > 1e-10)
                if qs < current_score - 1e-12:
                    p, current_score, current_c, current_Ff = q, qs, qc, qF
                    x_state = q.copy()
                    stagnation = 0
                    improved = True
                    if qs < best_score - 1e-13:
                        best_score, best_p = qs, p.copy()
                        pool.add(p, best_score)
                elif moved and qs <= current_score + 0.01 * current_score + 1e-12:
                    p, current_score, current_c, current_Ff = q, qs, qc, qF
                    x_state = q.copy()
                    stagnation += 1
                    if qs < best_score - 1e-13:
                        best_score, best_p = qs, p.copy()
                        pool.add(p, best_score)
                else:
                    stagnation += 2

            if not improved and stagnation >= 8:
                std = 0.04 + 0.01 * min(4.0, float(stagnation))
                noise = np.exp(self.rng.normal(0.0, std, size=self.n))
                p = self.clean(best_p * noise)
                current_score, current_c, current_Ff = self.score_p(p)
                x_state = p.copy()
                stagadation = 0
                stagnation = 0
                if current_score < best_score - 1e-13:
                    best_score, best_p = current_score, p.copy()
                    pool.add(p, best_score)

            if stagnation >= 18:
                std = 0.12
                noise = np.exp(self.rng.normal(0.0, std, size=self.n))
                p = self.clean(best_p * noise)
                current_score, current_c, current_Ff = self.score_p(p)
                x_state = p.copy()
                stagnation = 0

        return best_p, best_score


def _make_starts(n, parent, seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    starts = []
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)

    pf = _finite_list(parent)
    if pf is not None and pf.size >= n:
        p = np.asarray(pf[:n], dtype=np.float64)
        if np.isfinite(p).all() and float(p.sum()) > 0.0:
            starts.append(p)
            smooth = 0.0
            s = 1.0
            for m in range(1, 9):
                s += (0.25 / (m ** 0.7)) * np.cos(
                    2.0 * np.pi * m * x + rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
                )
            starts.append(p * np.exp(0.03 * s))

    for lam in (0.7, 1.1, 1.6, 2.4, 4.0):
        starts.append(np.exp(lam * x))

    # Asymmetric smooth random starts.
    for _ in range(7):
        s = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in range(1, 9):
            amp = 0.55 / (m ** 0.7)
            s = s + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * x + rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        starts.append(np.exp(0.65 * (1.0 + 0.5 * s)))

    # Two-level block starts (one-sided mass imbalance).
    for alpha in (0.35, 0.50, 0.65):
        p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        L1 = max(1, int(round(alpha * n)))
        L2 = max(1, n - L1)
        a = 2.0 / (alpha + 1.0)
        b = 1.0 / (alpha + 1.0)
        p[:L1] = a
        p[L1:] = b
        starts.append(p)

    # Bump starts.
    for ctr in (0.25, 0.45, 0.70):
        starts.append(1.0 + 1.8 * np.exp(-(((x - ctr) / 0.18) ** 2)))

    return starts


def _variants_for_eval(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    out = []
    if a.size > 0 and float(a.sum()) >= 0.01:
        out.append([float(v) for v in a])
    m = float(a.max())
    if m > 1e-15:
        b = a / m
        if b.size > 0 and float(b.sum()) >= 0.01 and float(b.max()) <= 1000.0:
            out.append([float(v) for v in b])
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s > 0.0:
        c = a / s
        if c.size > 0 and float(c.sum()) >= 0.01 and float(c.max()) <= 1000.0:
            out.append([float(v) for v in c])
    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1300.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0.0:
        budget = 1300.0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    hard_deadline = t0 + max(1.0, budget - 5.0)

    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    n = 6121
    pf = _finite_list(parent)
    if pf is not None and pf.size >= 64:
        n = int(min(pf.size, 6121))
    if budget < 120.0:
        n = int(min(n, 1024))
    n = max(1, int(n))

    opt_deadline = hard_deadline - min(120.0, max(40.0, 0.1 * budget))
    if opt_deadline < t0 + 10.0:
        opt_deadline = t0 + max(1.0, budget - 10.0)

    optimizer = _ConvOptimizer(n, seed=int(seed) if np.isfinite(seed) else 42)
    pool = _Pool(18)

    # Guaranteed fallback: constants attain exactly score 2 for the given evaluator.
    const_internal = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    pool.add(const_internal, 2.0)

    try:
        starts = _make_starts(n, parent, int(seed) if np.isfinite(seed) else 42)
    except Exception:
        starts = []

    # Score all cheap starts, keep promising ones.
    for p in starts:
        if time.monotonic() > opt_deadline:
            break
        try:
            pc = optimizer.clean(p)
            sc, _, _ = optimizer.score_p(pc)
            pool.add(pc, sc)
        except Exception:
            pass

    try:
        top = pool.top(8)
        # Prefer several good starts, plus a couple of later ones for diversity.
        selected = top[:4]
        if len(top) > 4:
            selected.extend(top[4:6])

        # If budget is large, give top candidates essentially all time.
        for i, (sc, p0) in enumerate(selected):
            rem = opt_deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 45.0:
                break
            remaining_after = len(selected) - i
            alloc = min(rem, rem / float(max(1, remaining_after)))
            one_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(45.0, alloc)
            if one_deadline > opt_deadline:
                one_deadline = opt_deadline
            try:
                optimizer.optimize_start(p0, one_deadline, pool)
            except Exception:
                pass

        # If time remains, polish the current best once more.
        best = pool.top(1)
        if best and time.monotonic() + 60.0 < opt_deadline:
            try:
                scorer, bp = best[0]
                if scorer < 2.0:
                    optimizer.optimize_start(bp, min(opt_deadline, time.monotonic() + (opt_deadline - time.monotonic()) * 1.0), pool)
            except Exception:
                pass
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Validate with the authoritative evaluator and pick the best exact score.
    final_deadline = hard_deadline
    best_exact = math.inf
    best_seq = [1.0] * n

    def consider_sequence(seq):
        nonlocal best_exact, best_seq
        if time.monotonic() > final_deadline:
            return
        if not seq:
            return
        try:
            s = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        except Exception:
            return
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_exact:
            best_exact = s
            best_seq = [float(v) for v in seq]

    # Good exact fallback first.
    consider_sequence([1.0] * n)

    # Then exact-check the best internal candidates in both useful normalizations.
    seen = set()
    for sc, a in sorted(pool.top(12), key=lambda z: z[0]):
        if time.monotonic() > final_deadline:
            break
        for seq in _variants_for_eval(a):
            key = tuple(seq[:5]) + (round(float(np.sum(seq)), 9), len(seq))
            if key in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(key)
            consider_sequence(seq)
            if time.monotonic() > final_deadline:
                break

    if not np.isfinite(best_exact) or not best_seq:
        best_seq = [1.0] * n

    return best_seq
```
